## ARCHITECTURE AUDIT

### CRITICAL Issues

**None found.**

---

### MAJOR Issues

**MAJOR-1 — `NormalizeDouble(lot, 2)` causes upward rounding for 3dp step symbols**

- Severity: MAJOR
- Root cause: Final `NormalizeDouble(lot, 2)` hardcodes 2 decimal places. For brokers with `SYMBOL_VOLUME_STEP = 0.001`, a floor-calculated lot of 0.078 rounds to 0.08 — 1 tick above the calculated value. Violates "never round lot upward" policy.
- Affected symbols: Brokers offering micro/nano lot steps (0.001, 0.0001)
- EURUSD/XAUUSD with step 0.01: not affected in standard brokers

**Exact patch:**

```mql5
// In _Risk_NormalizeLot() — replace both NormalizeDouble calls:

// Remove:
// lot = NormalizeDouble(lot, 8);   ← intermediate normalize, wrong precision
// return NormalizeDouble(lot, 2);  ← hardcoded 2dp, rounds up for 3dp steps

// Replace with:
int    vol_digits = 0;
double s          = step;
while(s < 1.0 - 0.000001) { s *= 10.0; vol_digits++; }

double lot = NormalizeDouble(MathFloor(raw_lot / step) * step, vol_digits);
```

---

**MAJOR-2 — `Risk_Close()` early-return when `!_Risk_Ready` prevents state clear**

- Severity: MAJOR
- Root cause: `if(!_Risk_Ready) return;` at top of `Risk_Close()` means that after a failed `Risk_Init()`, calling `Risk_Close()` does nothing — `halt_reason` and `symbol_economics_fail` persist. This contradicts T22A expectation that `Risk_GetInitStatus()` returns `"NOT_INITIALIZED"` after Close.
- Runtime impact: T22A FAIL. Also means a developer who calls `Risk_Close()` after failed init cannot cleanly re-init without residual halt state from previous attempt.

**Exact patch:** (stated above in C2 section — unconditional `_Risk_ZeroState()` in `Risk_Close()`)

---

**MAJOR-3 — `Diag_SetFlag()` silent failure when called before `Diag_Init()`**

- Severity: MAJOR (silent — no error surfaced)
- Root cause: `_Risk_SetHalt()` calls `Diag_SetFlag("trading_locked", true)`. If `Diag_Init()` has not been called, `Diag_SetFlag()` returns silently due to `if(!_Diag_Ready) return;` guard. The halt is set in RiskState correctly, but Diagnostics flag is not set — divergence between Risk halt state and Diagnostics flag state.
- Runtime impact: No crash. Wrong diagnostic output. Developer may see `trading_locked=false` in Diagnostics while Risk Engine is actually halted.

**Exact patch — add to `Risk_Init()` before any other call:**

```mql5
bool Risk_Init(string symbol, double account_balance)
{
    _Risk_Ready  = false;
    _Risk_Symbol = symbol;
    _Risk_ZeroState();

    // Guard: warn if Diagnostics not ready
    if(!Diag_IsReady())
        Log_Write("RISK", "WARN",
            "Risk_Init: Diag_IsReady()=false — "
            "Diag flags will not mirror halt state. "
            "Call Diag_Init() before Risk_Init().");

    // ... rest of init
}
```

---

### MINOR Issues

**MINOR-1 — `RISK_HALT_*` constants defined in implementation, not contract**

- Severity: MINOR
- Root cause: `Risk_Contract.mqh` documents the strings but does not `#define` them. Any module that needs to compare against these constants must include `RiskEngine_v1.mqh` rather than just the contract.
- Impact: Future consumers (PortfolioGuardian, Recovery Engine) must include full implementation to access constants — increases coupling.

**Recommended fix (Phase 4):** Move `#define RISK_HALT_*` constants into `Risk_Contract.mqh`.

---

**MINOR-2 — `_Risk_ZeroState()` clears `last_reset_day` to 0**

- Severity: MINOR
- Root cause: After `Risk_Close()` followed by `Risk_Init()` on same server day, `last_reset_day` is reset to current day in `Risk_Init()`. Correct. But if `Risk_Init()` is called on day 1, runs, `Risk_Close()` is called, then `Risk_Init()` is called again on day 1 — `last_reset_day` = day 1 both times → no new-day reset triggered. This is correct behavior but can confuse developers who expect re-init to trigger a reset.
- Impact: Cosmetic/documentation issue only.

---

**MINOR-3 — `Risk_ManualReset()` called when `!_Risk_Ready` returns silently**

- Severity: MINOR
- Root cause: `if(!_Risk_Ready) return;` at top of `Risk_ManualReset()`. If called after failed init (where halt flags are set), the manual reset silently does nothing — the developer cannot clear halt state from a failed init via `Risk_ManualReset()`.
- The correct path is `Risk_Close()` → `Risk_Init()`. This is acceptable behavior but should be documented.

---

## LOT CALCULATION AUDIT

### Formula verification:

```
value_per_point = tick_value × (point / tick_size)
sl_value_per_lot = sl_distance_points × value_per_point
raw_lot = risk_amount / sl_value_per_lot
```

### Cross-symbol validation:

**EURUSD** — Standard forex 5-digit

```
tick_value = 1.00 USD per tick (0.00001)
tick_size  = 0.00001
point      = 0.00001
point/tick_size = 1.0
value_per_point = 1.00 × 1.0 = 1.00 USD/point/lot
SL = 150 points, risk = $100
sl_value_per_lot = 150 × 1.00 = $150/lot
raw_lot = 100 / 150 = 0.667 → floor to 0.01 step → 0.66 lot ✓
```

**XAUUSD** — Gold CFD

```
tick_value = 1.00 USD per tick (0.01)
tick_size  = 0.01
point      = 0.01
point/tick_size = 1.0
value_per_point = 1.00 × 1.0 = 1.00 USD/point/lot
SL = 150 points (= $1.50/oz move), risk = $100
sl_value_per_lot = 150 × 1.00 = $150/lot
raw_lot = 100 / 150 = 0.667 → 0.66 lot ✓
```

**EURUSD via 4-digit broker** — Where `point = 0.0001`, `tick_size = 0.0001`

```
tick_value = 10.00 USD per tick
tick_size  = 0.0001
point      = 0.0001
point/tick_size = 1.0
value_per_point = 10.00 × 1.0 = 10.00 USD/point/lot
SL = 15 pips = 150 points (0.0001 each)
sl_value_per_lot = 150 × 10.00 = $1500/lot
raw_lot = 100 / 1500 = 0.067 → 0.06 lot ✓
```

**BTCUSD** — Crypto CFD — point ≠ tick_size case

```
SYMBOL_POINT     = 0.01  (smallest price increment)
SYMBOL_TICK_SIZE = 0.01  (matches point for most crypto CFDs)
tick_value       = varies by broker (e.g. $0.01 per tick per 1 BTC lot)
point/tick_size  = 1.0

Some brokers define BTCUSD:
  tick_size  = 1.00   (1 USD per tick)
  tick_value = 1.00   (1 USD per 1 USD move, 1 BTC lot)
  point      = 0.01
  point/tick_size = 0.01/1.00 = 0.01
  value_per_point = 1.00 × 0.01 = 0.01 USD/point/lot

SL = 500 points (= $5.00 move), risk = $100
sl_value_per_lot = 500 × 0.01 = $5.00/lot
raw_lot = 100 / 5 = 20.0 BTC  ← CORRECT for this broker definition
```

**CONCLUSION — Formula is mathematically correct** for all standard symbol types. The key invariant is that `tick_value / tick_size` gives USD per point per lot in the account currency. The formula correctly handles the `point ≠ tick_size` case via the `point/tick_size` ratio.

**LOT CALCULATION AUDIT: PASS** (subject to broker providing correct tick_value in account currency)

|------|---------------|--------|

## FINAL PATCH LIST (Required before Phase 3 DoD)

### Patch A — `_Risk_NormalizeLot()` — fix upward rounding (MAJOR-1)

```mql5
static double _Risk_NormalizeLot(double raw_lot, string symbol)
{
    double step = SymbolInfoDouble(symbol, SYMBOL_VOLUME_STEP);
    double vmin = SymbolInfoDouble(symbol, SYMBOL_VOLUME_MIN);
    double vmax = SymbolInfoDouble(symbol, SYMBOL_VOLUME_MAX);

    if(step <= 0.0) step = 0.01;

    // Derive precision from step — no hardcoded decimal places
    int    vol_digits = 0;
    double s          = step;
    while(s < 1.0 - 0.000001) { s *= 10.0; vol_digits++; }

    // Round DOWN only — single normalize call at correct precision
    double lot = NormalizeDouble(MathFloor(raw_lot / step) * step, vol_digits);

    // Reject if below any minimum — never raise
    if(lot < vmin - 0.000001)
    {
        Log_Write("RISK", "WARN",
            "NormalizeLot REJECT  lot=" + DoubleToString(lot, vol_digits) +
            " < vol_min=" + DoubleToString(vmin, vol_digits) +
            "  raw=" + DoubleToString(raw_lot, vol_digits) +
            " — rejected, not raised");
        return 0.0;
    }

    if(lot < inp_Risk_MinLotSize - 0.000001)
    {
        Log_Write("RISK", "WARN",
            "NormalizeLot REJECT  lot=" + DoubleToString(lot, vol_digits) +
            " < inp_Risk_MinLotSize=" + DoubleToString(inp_Risk_MinLotSize, vol_digits) +
            "  raw=" + DoubleToString(raw_lot, vol_digits) +
            " — rejected, not raised");
        return 0.0;
    }

    // Clamp downward against max only
    lot = MathMin(lot, inp_Risk_MaxLotSize);
    if(vmax > 0.0)
        lot = MathMin(lot, vmax);

    return NormalizeDouble(lot, vol_digits);
}
```

### Patch B — `Risk_Close()` — unconditional state clear (MAJOR-2)

```mql5
void Risk_Close()
{
    if(_Risk_Ready)
    {
        Log_Write("RISK", "INFO",
            "Risk_Close" +
            "  daily_dd="    + DoubleToString(_Risk_State.daily_dd_pct, 2) + "%" +
            "  total_dd="    + DoubleToString(_Risk_State.total_dd_pct, 2) + "%" +
            "  daily_halt="  + (_Risk_State.daily_halt             ? "Y" : "N") +
            "  total_halt="  + (_Risk_State.total_halt             ? "Y" : "N") +
            "  manual_halt=" + (_Risk_State.manual_halt            ? "Y" : "N") +
            "  sym_econ="    + (_Risk_State.symbol_economics_fail  ? "FAIL" : "ok"));
    }
    else if(_Risk_State.halt_reason != "")
    {
        Log_Write("RISK", "INFO",
            "Risk_Close (after failed init)" +
            "  halt_reason=" + _Risk_State.halt_reason);
    }

    // Unconditional — always reset, regardless of _Risk_Ready
    _Risk_Ready  = false;
    _Risk_Symbol = "";
    _Risk_ZeroState();
}
```

- ownership ambiguity  
  
3. FAILURE CONTAINMENT

# PHASE 4 — EA_TEMPLATE Architecture Review
## Pre-Implementation Validation Report

---

## 1. COMPILE AUDIT

### Include Chain Projection — Phase 4

```
EA_TEMPLATE.mq5
  ├── Log_Contract.mqh           (no deps)
  ├── Logging_v1.mqh             (← Log_Contract)
  ├── Diagnostics_Contract.mqh   (no deps)
  ├── Diagnostics_v1.mqh         (← Diagnostics_Contract, Logging_v1)
  ├── Risk_Contract.mqh          (no deps)
  ├── RiskEngine_v1.mqh          (← Risk_Contract, Logging_v1, Diagnostics_v1)
  ├── Signal_Contract.mqh        (no deps — Phase 5)
  ├── [Signal_vX.mqh]            (← Signal_Contract)
  └── EA_Lifecycle.mqh           (← all above)
```

**Duplicate include risk:**

```
Logging_v1.mqh included by:
  - EA_TEMPLATE.mq5 directly
  - Diagnostics_v1.mqh
  - RiskEngine_v1.mqh
  → 3 include paths → #ifndef __LOGGING_V1__ guard required ✓ (present)

Diagnostics_v1.mqh included by:
  - EA_TEMPLATE.mq5 directly
  - RiskEngine_v1.mqh
  → 2 include paths → #ifndef __DIAGNOSTICS_V1__ guard required ✓ (present)
```

**ISSUE FOUND — `EA_Lifecycle.mqh` include position:**

`EA_Lifecycle.mqh` will orchestrate all module calls. If it `#include`s module headers itself, it creates a second include chain parallel to EA_TEMPLATE's explicit includes. This risks:
- Guard collision if module header is included in both EA_TEMPLATE and EA_Lifecycle
- State initialization ambiguity — which file "owns" the include?

**Decision required before Phase 4 implementation:**

> `EA_Lifecycle.mqh` must NOT include any module headers directly. It must assume all modules are already included by EA_TEMPLATE.mq5 before EA_Lifecycle.mqh is processed. EA_Lifecycle is a coordinator, not an includer.

**RISK_HALT_* constants — currently defined in RiskEngine_v1.mqh:**

EA_Lifecycle.mqh will need to compare halt reasons for routing decisions. It must include RiskEngine_v1.mqh to access these constants. This is acceptable — but if constants are ever needed in Signal or Execution modules without including full RiskEngine, they are inaccessible.

**Recommended fix (MINOR-1 from Phase 3, now elevated):**

Move `#define RISK_HALT_*` from `RiskEngine_v1.mqh` to `Risk_Contract.mqh`. Any module that needs halt reason strings includes only the lightweight contract, not the full implementation.

**Compile Audit Verdict: CONDITIONAL PASS**
Requires EA_Lifecycle include policy decision before Phase 4 build begins.

---

## 2. RUNTIME LIFECYCLE AUDIT

### Proposed Init Order Validation

```
OnInit():
  1. Log_Init()       ← no deps          ✓
  2. Diag_Init()      ← needs Logging    ✓
  3. Risk_Init()      ← needs Logging + Diagnostics  ✓
  4. Signal_Init()    ← needs Logging + Diagnostics  ✓ (Phase 5)
  5. Execution_Init() ← needs all above  ✓ (future)
```

**Ordering is correct.** Each module's dependencies are initialized before it.

### Partial Init Failure Propagation — CRITICAL GAP

Current pattern has no defined behavior for partial init failure:

```mql5
// Current implicit pattern (no EA_Lifecycle yet):
Log_Init(...);
Diag_Init(...);
bool risk_ok = Risk_Init(...);
// What happens if risk_ok = false?
// Signal_Init() is called anyway?
// EA enters READY state anyway?
// No defined answer exists.
```

**This is the most dangerous gap in Phase 4.** Without a centralized lifecycle state machine, partial init leaves the EA in an undefined state — some modules ready, some not — with no consistent halt behavior.

**Required: Explicit init state machine in `EA_Lifecycle.mqh`:**

```mql5
// EA_Lifecycle.mqh — proposed init sequence
bool EA_InitAll(string symbol, string ea_name, int magic)
{
    g_EA_State = EA_STATE_INIT;

    Log_Init(ea_name, symbol);
    Diag_Init(ea_name, symbol);

    if(!Risk_Init(symbol, AccountInfoDouble(ACCOUNT_BALANCE)))
    {
        Log_Write("EA", "ERROR",
            "Init FAILED at Risk_Init — halting");
        Diag_SetFlag("trading_locked", true);
        g_EA_State = EA_STATE_ERROR;
        return false;
    }

    // Signal_Init() — Phase 5
    // Execution_Init() — future

    g_EA_State = EA_STATE_READY;
    Log_Write("EA", "INFO",
        "Init complete  state=READY  magic=" + IntegerToString(magic));
    return true;
}
```

**Key rule:** If ANY module init returns false → halt immediately, do not init subsequent modules, set `g_EA_State = EA_STATE_ERROR`.

### Deinit Order Validation

```
OnDeinit():
  5. Execution_Close()  ← close positions/orders first
  4. Signal_Close()     ← release indicator handles
  3. Risk_Close()       ← flush risk state
  2. Diag_Close()       ← export CSV/TXT, flush diagnostic
  1. Log_Close()        ← flush log file — MUST be last
```

**Ordering is correct — reverse of init.**

**ISSUE — Diag_Close() exports CSV before Log_Close() flushes log:**

`Diag_Close()` calls `Diag_ExportSummary()` which calls `Log_Write()` internally. This is fine only if `Log_Close()` has not been called yet. Current ordering (Diag before Log) is correct. ✓

**ISSUE — Risk_Close() after failed init:**

After Phase 3 Patch B, `Risk_Close()` is unconditional — safe to call even after failed init. ✓

**ISSUE — OnDeinit `reason` parameter not used:**

MT5 passes deinit reason (REASON_REMOVE, REASON_CHARTCLOSE, REASON_RECOMPILE, etc.). Current architecture ignores this. For production:

| Reason | Recommended behavior |
|--------|---------------------|
| `REASON_REMOVE` | Full close — close positions if configured |
| `REASON_RECOMPILE` | Do NOT close positions — EA will reload |
| `REASON_CHARTCLOSE` | Full close |
| `REASON_ACCOUNT` | Full close |

**Required addition to EA_Lifecycle:**

```mql5
void EA_DeinitAll(const int reason)
{
    bool close_positions = (reason != REASON_RECOMPILE &&
                            reason != REASON_PARAMETERS);

    // Execution_Close(close_positions);  // future
    // Signal_Close();                    // future
    Risk_Close();
    Diag_Close();
    Log_Write("EA", "INFO",
        "Deinit  reason=" + IntegerToString(reason) +
        "  close_positions=" + (close_positions ? "Y" : "N"));
    Log_Close();
}
```

### OnTick Pipeline Validation

**Proposed order:**

```
OnTick():
  1. Diag_Heartbeat()       ← update cycle counter
  2. Risk_UpdateState()     ← recalculate DD, check new day
  3. Signal_Update()        ← evaluate signal
  4. Risk_CheckHalt()       ← gate before execution
  5. Execution_Process()    ← entry/exit logic
```

**ISSUE — Heavy logic in OnTick:**

OnTick fires on every price change — potentially hundreds of times per second on live accounts. Running `Risk_UpdateState()`, `Signal_Update()`, and `Execution_Process()` on every tick is a known MT5 performance problem.

**Required: Timer-based pipeline instead of raw OnTick:**

```mql5
// EA_TEMPLATE.mq5
int OnInit()
{
    if(!EA_InitAll(...)) return INIT_FAILED;
    EventSetTimer(1);   // 1-second timer cycle
    return INIT_SUCCEEDED;
}

void OnTick()
{
    // OnTick only: update price cache (bid/ask)
    // No heavy logic here
    g_Current_Bid = SymbolInfoDouble(_Symbol, SYMBOL_BID);
    g_Current_Ask = SymbolInfoDouble(_Symbol, SYMBOL_ASK);
}

void OnTimer()
{
    if(g_EA_State != EA_STATE_READY &&
       g_EA_State != EA_STATE_IN_TRADE) return;

    Diag_Increment("timer_cycles");
    Risk_UpdateState(AccountInfoDouble(ACCOUNT_EQUITY));

    if(Risk_CheckHalt())
    {
        Diag_SetFlag("trading_locked", true);
        return;
    }

    // Signal_Update();        // Phase 5
    // Execution_Process();    // future
}

void OnDeinit(const int reason) { EA_DeinitAll(reason); EventKillTimer(); }
```

**This is the correct MT5 architecture for a robust production EA.**

---

## 3. ARCHITECTURE AUDIT

### State Ownership Analysis

| Module | State location | Owner | Risk |
|--------|---------------|-------|------|
| Logging_v1 | `static _Log_*` in .mqh | Self | Low — no external writes |
| Diagnostics_v1 | `static _Diag_*` in .mqh | Self | Low — external via setters only |
| RiskEngine_v1 | `static _Risk_*` in .mqh | Self | Medium — external via Risk_ManualReset |
| EA_TEMPLATE | `g_EA_State` in .mq5 | EA_TEMPLATE | Required |

**Hidden coupling found — Diagnostics ↔ Risk:**

`RiskEngine_v1.mqh` calls `Diag_SetFlag()` and `Diag_RecordBlock()` directly. This means:
- Risk Engine has a **write dependency** on Diagnostics
- Diagnostics must be initialized before Risk — enforced by init order
- But this coupling is **invisible from the contract** — `Risk_Contract.mqh` does not document this dependency

**Not a bug — but a documentation gap.** If a future developer reads `Risk_Contract.mqh` and tries to use RiskEngine without Diagnostics, it will fail silently (Diag guards return quietly).

**Required addition to `Risk_Contract.mqh`:**

```markdown
# Runtime Dependencies (not include dependencies)
RiskEngine_v1 calls Diag_SetFlag() and Diag_RecordBlock() at runtime.
Diagnostics_v1 must be initialized (Diag_Init() called) before Risk_Init().
If Diagnostics is not ready, halt flags will not mirror in diagnostic output.
```

### EA State Machine — Required

No state machine currently exists at the EA level. This is the most important Phase 4 deliverable.

**Proposed `ENUM_EA_STATE`:**

```mql5
enum ENUM_EA_STATE
{
    EA_STATE_UNINITIALIZED = 0,
    EA_STATE_INIT          = 1,
    EA_STATE_READY         = 2,
    EA_STATE_IN_TRADE      = 3,
    EA_STATE_HALTED        = 4,
    EA_STATE_ERROR         = 5,
    EA_STATE_CLOSING       = 6
};
```

**Transition rules:**

| From | To | Trigger |
|------|----|---------|
| UNINITIALIZED | INIT | OnInit() called |
| INIT | READY | All modules init OK |
| INIT | ERROR | Any module init fails |
| READY | IN_TRADE | Position opened |
| READY | HALTED | Risk_CheckHalt() = true |
| IN_TRADE | READY | Position closed |
| IN_TRADE | HALTED | Risk_CheckHalt() = true mid-trade |
| HALTED | READY | Halt cleared (new day / manual reset) |
| ANY | CLOSING | OnDeinit() called |
| ANY | ERROR | Unhandled exception / module failure |

**`g_EA_State` must be declared in `EA_TEMPLATE.mq5` — not in any `.mqh`.**

---

## 4. MT5 RUNTIME AUDIT

### Issue: Static Variable Persistence Across Tester Runs

**MAJOR MT5-SPECIFIC RISK:**

In Strategy Tester optimization mode, MT5 runs multiple passes **in the same process**. Static variables in `.mqh` files may **persist between optimization passes** if the guard prevents re-initialization.

```mql5
// Risk scenario:
// Pass 1: Risk_Init() called → _Risk_Ready = true, _Risk_State populated
// Pass 1: Risk_Close() called → _Risk_Ready = false, state zeroed ✓
// Pass 2: Risk_Init() called → _Risk_Ready = false → init runs correctly ✓
```

**Post Phase 3 Patch B (unconditional `_Risk_ZeroState()` in `Risk_Close()`):** This risk is mitigated — each pass gets a clean state. ✓

**But Diagnostics_v1 has the same pattern — verify:**

`Diag_Close()` sets `_Diag_Ready = false` and calls `_Diag_ZeroCounters()` + `_Diag_ZeroFlags()`. ✓ Safe.

**Logging_v1 — file handle persistence:**

If `Log_Close()` is not called between optimization passes (because tester doesn't trigger `OnDeinit()` in some configurations), `_Log_Handle` could remain open. `Log_Init()` would then call `FileOpen()` again without closing the old handle → **handle leak**.

**Required fix in `Log_Init()`:**

```mql5
void Log_Init(string ea_name, string symbol)
{
    // Close existing handle if already open (tester re-run safety)
    if(_Log_Handle != INVALID_HANDLE)
    {
        FileFlush(_Log_Handle);
        FileClose(_Log_Handle);
        _Log_Handle = INVALID_HANDLE;
    }
    _Log_Ready = false;
    // ... rest of init
}
```

### Issue: `TimeCurrent()` in Strategy Tester

`TimeCurrent()` in Strategy Tester returns the current bar's open time — **not real-time**. This means:

- Daily DD reset based on day-of-year comparison will function correctly in tester — it advances with bar time ✓
- But in **Open Prices Only** mode, `TimeCurrent()` only updates at bar open — daily reset will only be detected at the first tick of a new day bar ✓ (acceptable)

### Issue: `AccountInfoDouble(ACCOUNT_EQUITY)` in Tester

In Strategy Tester, `ACCOUNT_EQUITY` reflects modeled positions. This is correct for DD tracking. ✓

### Issue: `OnTick()` Reentrancy

MQL5 does not support true multithreading — `OnTick()` is not reentrant. However, if `OnTimer()` and `OnTick()` both fire and the timer handler is slow, MT5 queues the events. This is not a reentrancy risk but can cause **timer event backlog** on slow systems.

**Mitigation already in design:** heavy logic in `OnTimer()`, `OnTick()` only updates price cache. ✓

### Issue: Symbol Property Caching

`SymbolInfoDouble(symbol, SYMBOL_TRADE_TICK_VALUE)` is called on **every** `Risk_CalculateLot()` invocation. For a 1-second timer this is 86,400 calls per day. This is a live broker API call — not cached.

**Recommended: Cache symbol economics at Risk_Init() time:**

```mql5
// In RiskState struct — add cached economics:
struct RiskState {
    // ... existing fields ...
    double cached_tick_value;
    double cached_tick_size;
    double cached_point;
    double cached_vol_min;
    double cached_vol_max;
    double cached_vol_step;
    int    cached_vol_digits;
};

// Populate in Risk_Init() after Risk_CheckSymbolEconomics() passes.
// Re-validate periodically (e.g., every 1000 cycles) or on symbol change.
```

This reduces live API calls from every lot calculation to once at init (plus periodic revalidation). Minor performance improvement — but correct behavior in environments where broker properties can change mid-session (rare but possible on reconnect).

---

## 5. SCALABILITY AUDIT

### Q1: Is current module ownership model scalable to 10+ modules?

**Answer: YES, with one constraint.**

Static-per-module pattern scales to any number of modules as long as each module has exactly one `.mqh` file with proper `#ifndef` guards. 10 modules = 10 independent static namespaces. No interference.

**The constraint:** Each module's static state is invisible to other modules except through the public getter API. This is the correct design. It must be enforced as a platform rule — no direct `_ModuleName_*` access from outside the owning file.

---

### Q2: Is Pattern A still correct for future EA scale?

**Answer: YES for Phase 4–7. Revisit at Phase 8+ (PortfolioGuardian automation).**

Pattern A (struct + naming convention) is appropriate for MQL5's limitations. The alternative — Pattern B (abstract base class + virtual functions) — adds virtual dispatch overhead and is unnecessary when the primary variation point is the Signal module (swapped at compile time, not runtime).

**Exception:** If PortfolioGuardian needs to dynamically manage N different EAs' risk states at runtime (not compile time), Pattern B or a registry pattern may be required. Not needed now.

---

### Q3: Should any state move from implementation → contract?

**Answer: One specific item — YES.**

`RISK_HALT_*` string constants should move from `RiskEngine_v1.mqh` to `Risk_Contract.mqh`. All other state correctly lives in implementation files.

No struct definitions should move to contracts — that would recreate the coupling problem that Pattern A is designed to avoid.

---

### Q4: Is centralized lifecycle orchestration sufficient?

**Answer: YES, via `EA_Lifecycle.mqh` + EA state machine in `EA_TEMPLATE.mq5`.**

The EA_TEMPLATE owns `g_EA_State`. EA_Lifecycle provides `EA_InitAll()` and `EA_DeinitAll()` as pure coordinators. This is sufficient for Phase 4–9. PortfolioGuardian will add a second layer of lifecycle management across multiple EA instances — but that is out of scope for EA_TEMPLATE itself.

---

### Q5: Is init-failure handling production-safe?

**Answer: NOT YET — will be production-safe after Phase 4 adds the state machine.**

Current gap: no centralized init failure response. Phase 4 must implement: if any module init fails → `g_EA_State = EA_STATE_ERROR` → no further processing → `return INIT_FAILED` from `OnInit()`.

---

### Q6: Are any current APIs dangerous long-term?

**Answer: Two APIs warrant attention.**

**API-1 — `Diag_SetFlag(string flag_name, bool value)`:**
String-keyed setter is convenient but brittle — a typo in `flag_name` silently does nothing (WARN logged, but no compile error). At 10+ modules calling `Diag_SetFlag()` this becomes a maintenance risk.

Acceptable for Phase 4. If more than 20 distinct flag-setting call sites accumulate, consider typed enum-keyed setters in a future Diagnostics_v2.

**API-2 — `Risk_ManualReset(bool, bool, bool)`:**
Boolean parameter list with no names at call site: `Risk_ManualReset(true, false, false)` — unclear which halt is being reset without reading the signature. At scale this is a readability problem.

**Recommended Phase 4 addition — named bit flags:**

```mql5
// Risk_Contract.mqh
#define RISK_RESET_TOTAL_HALT       1
#define RISK_RESET_MANUAL_HALT      2
#define RISK_RESET_SYMBOL_ECONOMICS 4

// Call site:
Risk_ManualReset(RISK_RESET_TOTAL_HALT | RISK_RESET_MANUAL_HALT);

// Implementation:
void Risk_ManualReset(int flags)
{
    bool reset_total   = (flags & RISK_RESET_TOTAL_HALT)       != 0;
    bool reset_manual  = (flags & RISK_RESET_MANUAL_HALT)      != 0;
    bool reset_sym_eco = (flags & RISK_RESET_SYMBOL_ECONOMICS) != 0;
    // ... existing logic unchanged
}
```

This is a clean API improvement with zero architectural change. Implement at Phase 4 or defer to RiskEngine_v1.1.

---

### Q7: Is static-state-per-module safe for multi-symbol / multi-instance / VPS?

**Answer: SAFE for single-symbol single-instance. NOT SAFE for multi-instance on same chart.**

| Deployment | Safe? | Reason |
|-----------|-------|--------|
| Single EA, single symbol, single chart | ✓ YES | One static namespace per module |
| Single EA, single symbol, VPS (Linux Wine MT5) | ✓ YES | Process isolation — no shared memory |
| Two EAs on different charts, same terminal | ✓ YES | Each EA runs in its own MQL5 thread with isolated static state |
| Two instances of SAME EA on same chart | ✗ NO | Cannot happen in MT5 — MT5 prevents duplicate EA on same chart |
| Multi-symbol EA (one EA, multiple symbols in OnTick) | ✗ NO | Static symbol state (`_Risk_Symbol`) is shared — only one symbol supported per instance |

**Multi-symbol risk is the most important constraint to document:**

`_Risk_Symbol` and all economics cache values are single-string static variables. A multi-symbol EA calling `Risk_CalculateLot()` for different symbols in sequence would use the same cached state for all. This is **by design** for single-symbol EAs but **must be documented as a hard platform constraint**.

---

### Q8: What breaks first at scale?

**In order of likelihood:**

| Rank | What breaks | Trigger |
|------|-------------|---------|
| 1 | `Diag_SetFlag()` silent failures | 10+ modules calling with typos — no compile safety |
| 2 | Log file size | Long-running EA with DEBUG level — unbounded growth |
| 3 | Symbol economics cache staleness | Broker reconnect mid-session changes tick_value |
| 4 | `Risk_ManualReset()` parameter confusion | Multiple developers, no named parameters |
| 5 | Include chain compile time | 15+ module files — MQL5 compile becomes slow |
| 6 | Static state in tester optimization | If `Log_Init()` handle leak not fixed (Phase 3 item) |

---

### Q9: What should be fixed BEFORE PortfolioGuardian?

**Priority order:**

```
1. Phase 4: EA_TEMPLATE state machine (g_EA_State) — required for
   PortfolioGuardian to query EA operational state

2. Log_Init() handle leak fix — required for optimization runs

3. RISK_HALT_* constants moved to Risk_Contract.mqh — required for
   PortfolioGuardian to read halt reasons without full Risk include

4. Symbol economics caching in RiskState — required for performance
   at scale (PortfolioGuardian may query multiple EAs rapidly)

5. Risk_ManualReset() named flag API — required before PortfolioGuardian
   automates halt resets
```

---

### Q10: What should NEVER be added to this architecture?

| Never add | Reason |
|-----------|--------|
| Global cross-module event bus / callback registry | MQL5 has no function pointers — any implementation would be fragile string-dispatch |
| Runtime module loading / plugin system | MQL5 compiles statically — no dynamic loading |
| Shared memory between EA instances | MT5 provides no shared memory API — use files or global variables only |
| Thread-safety primitives | MQL5 is single-threaded per EA — mutexes are unnecessary and unavailable |
| Database connections from EA | Latency risk in OnTimer — use file export + external process |
| Automatic position sizing from Signal Engine | Lot sizing is Risk Engine's exclusive domain — never delegate |
| Recovery logic in Entry/Exit modules | Recovery Engine is optional and separate — never merge |
| GUI panels with user input that modifies risk parameters at runtime | Creates unauditable state changes — use input parameters only |

---

## 6. CRITICAL ISSUES

**CRITICAL-1 — No EA-level state machine exists**

Without `g_EA_State` and `EA_InitAll()` returning false on module failure, the EA can enter a trading state with partially initialized modules. This is the Phase 4 primary deliverable — not optional.

**CRITICAL-2 — No partial init failure containment**

If `Risk_Init()` returns false and the EA continues to `Signal_Init()` then `OnTimer()`, the Risk Engine will be queried in an unready state. `Risk_CheckHalt()` returns true when `!_Risk_Ready` — this prevents trades, but the EA will spin in HALTED state with no recovery path and no clear error surface. Needs explicit ERROR state and `return INIT_FAILED`.

---

## 7. MAJOR ISSUES

**MAJOR-1 — `Log_Init()` handle leak in optimization mode**

Exact patch provided in Section 4. Required before any optimization run.

**MAJOR-2 — `OnDeinit` reason not used**

EA will close positions on recompile — potentially at wrong price or wrong time. `REASON_RECOMPILE` and `REASON_PARAMETERS` must not trigger position close. Patch provided in Section 2.

**MAJOR-3 — Symbol economics not cached**

`SymbolInfoDouble()` called on every lot calculation. Safe for development. Must be cached before live deployment at scale. Phase 4 acceptable to defer; must be done before live launch.

**MAJOR-4 — `Risk_ManualReset()` boolean parameter API**

Readability and maintenance risk at scale. Named bit-flag API patch provided in Section 5 Q6. Recommended at Phase 4; mandatory before PortfolioGuardian.

---

## 8. MINOR ISSUES

**MINOR-1 — `RISK_HALT_*` constants in wrong file**

Move from `RiskEngine_v1.mqh` to `Risk_Contract.mqh`. One-line change per constant. No logic change.

**MINOR-2 — Runtime dependency between Risk and Diagnostics not documented in contract**

Add dependency note to `Risk_Contract.mqh`. Documentation only.

**MINOR-3 — `EA_Lifecycle.mqh` include policy not yet defined**

Must be defined before Phase 4 implementation begins. Decision: EA_Lifecycle is a coordinator, not an includer. See Section 1.

**MINOR-4 — Log file rotation not implemented**

For long-running live EAs, daily log files will grow unbounded. Acceptable for Phase 4. Must be addressed before extended live deployment (weeks/months).

---

## 9. REQUIRED PATCHES BEFORE PHASE 4 BUILD

| # | Patch | File | Blocking? |
|---|-------|------|-----------|
| P1 | `Log_Init()` handle leak fix | `Logging_v1.mqh` | YES — optimization safety |
| P2 | Move `RISK_HALT_*` to `Risk_Contract.mqh` | Both files | YES — PortfolioGuardian prep |
| P3 | `Risk_ManualReset()` named flag API | `RiskEngine_v1.mqh` + `Risk_Contract.mqh` | RECOMMENDED |
| P4 | Document Risk→Diagnostics runtime dependency | `Risk_Contract.mqh` | NO — documentation only |
| P5 | Define EA_Lifecycle include policy | Architecture decision | YES — before build |

---

## 10. RECOMMENDED FUTURE CONSTRAINTS

These are platform-level rules that must be documented in `PLATFORM_RULES.md` before PortfolioGuardian development:

```
CONSTRAINT 1:
EA_TEMPLATE is single-symbol only.
_Risk_Symbol, _Diag_Symbol, _Log_FilePath are single static values.
Multi-symbol support requires a new architecture review.

CONSTRAINT 2:
EA_Lifecycle.mqh is a coordinator only — it calls module functions but
never includes module headers. All includes live in EA_TEMPLATE.mq5.

CONSTRAINT 3:
g_EA_State is owned exclusively by EA_TEMPLATE.mq5.
No module file (.mqh) may declare or modify g_EA_State directly.
EA_Lifecycle.mqh reads and writes g_EA_State via the EA_TEMPLATE scope.

CONSTRAINT 4:
PortfolioGuardian communicates with EA instances via:
  - GlobalVariableSet/Get (for real-time halt signals)
  - File-based CSV export from Diagnostics_v1
  - Magic number grouping of open positions
Never via direct function calls between EA processes.

CONSTRAINT 5:
No module may call another module's internal (_ModuleName_*) functions.
All cross-module communication is via public (ModuleName_*) API only.
This is enforced by code review — not by the compiler.

CONSTRAINT 6:
Log_Close() must always be the last call in OnDeinit().
No module may write to log after Log_Close() is called.
EA_DeinitAll() enforces this ordering.
```

----- [user] -----
เห็นด้วยกับ Phase 4 review ครับ
ก่อน build Phase 4 ให้ทำ Patch P1–P5 ก่อน โดยลำดับนี้:
1. Patch P1 — แก้ Log_Init() handle leak
- ถ้า _Log_Handle เปิดอยู่ ให้ FileFlush + FileClose ก่อนเปิดใหม่
- สำคัญสำหรับ optimization / tester rerun
2. Patch P2 — ย้าย RISK_HALT_* constants ไปไว้ใน Risk_Contract.mqh
- RiskEngine_v1.mqh ให้ remove duplicate #define
- เพื่อให้ future modules ใช้ constants ได้โดยไม่ต้อง include full implementation
3. Patch P3 — เปลี่ยน Risk_ManualReset(bool,bool,bool) เป็น named bit-flag API
- ใช้ RISK_RESET_TOTAL_HALT
- RISK_RESET_MANUAL_HALT
- RISK_RESET_SYMBOL_ECONOMICS
- ลดความเสี่ยง call ผิดตอน PortfolioGuardian ใช้
4. Patch P4 — เพิ่มเอกสาร runtime dependency ใน Risk_Contract.mqh
- RiskEngine depends on Diagnostics at runtime
- Required order: Log_Init → Diag_Init → Risk_Init
5. Patch P5 — Lock EA_Lifecycle include policy
- EA_Lifecycle.mqh must NOT include module headers
- EA_TEMPLATE.mq5 owns all includes
- EA_Lifecycle only coordinates calls
หลัง patch แล้วให้ compile/test Phase 1–3 อีกครั้งก่อน build Phase 4.
หลัง patch ผ่าน ค่อย build:
1. EA_Lifecycle.mqh
2. EA_TEMPLATE.mq5
3. Integration test without Signal/Execution
Do not build Signal, Entry, Exit, Recovery, Telegram, or PortfolioGuardian yet.

Risk_Close()\\n    2. Diag_Close()\\n    3. It orchestrates module function\\ncalls. It does NOT include module headers.\\n\\nRules:\\n- EA_TEMPLATE.mq5 is the ONLY file that includes module .mqh files\\n- EA_Lifecycle.mqh assumes all module functions are already available\\n  in scope via EA_TEMPLATE.mq5&#x27;s include chain\\n- EA_Lifecycle.mqh contains ONLY: function calls, state transitions,\\n  g_EA_State management, and init/deinit sequencing\\n- EA_Lifecycle.mqh must NOT contain: #include, struct definitions,\\n  input parameters, static variables, or module-level logic\\n\\nRationale:\\n- Prevents include chain duplication (Logging_v1 included in both\\n  EA_TEMPLATE and EA_Lifecycle would require additional guards)\\n- EA_TEMPLATE.mq5 is the single source of truth for the include graph\\n- EA_Lifecycle.mqh remains a thin orchestration layer \\u2014 testable in\\n  isolation by providing stub module functions\\n\\nEnforcement:\\n- Code review gate: EA_Lifecycle.mqh must have zero #include directives\\n- Any module-level call added to EA_Lifecycle ## Summary — P1–P5 Applied

### P1 — `Logging_v1.mqh` — Handle Leak Fix

```mql5
// Log_Init() ตอนนี้ปิด handle เก่าก่อนเสมอ
if(_Log_Handle != INVALID_HANDLE)
{
    FileFlush(_Log_Handle);
    FileClose(_Log_Handle);
    _Log_Handle = INVALID_HANDLE;
}
_Log_Ready    = false;
_Log_FilePath = "";
// ... เปิด handle ใหม่ตามปกติ
```
ป้องกัน handle leak ใน optimization pass และ tester re-run

// Risk_Contract.mqh — ตอนนี้ define ทั้งหมด

### P3 — `Risk_ManualReset()` → Named Bit-Flag API

| Old | New |
|-----|-----|
| `Risk_ManualReset(true, false, false)` | `Risk_ManualReset(RISK_RESET_TOTAL_HALT)` |
| `Risk_ManualReset(false, true, true)` | `Risk_ManualReset(RISK_RESET_MANUAL_HALT \| RISK_RESET_SYMBOL_ECONOMICS)` |
| `Risk_ManualReset(false, false, false)` | `Risk_ManualReset(0)` |

Test call sites ที่ต้องอัปเดต: T07, T10, T11, T15, T22

---

### P4 — `Risk_Contract.mqh` — Runtime Dependency Documentation

เพิ่ม section ชัดเจน:
- Required init order: `Log_Init → Diag_Init → Risk_Init`
- Required deinit order: `Risk_Close → Diag_Close → Log_Close`
- Runtime write deps: `Diag_SetFlag()`, `Diag_RecordBlock()` ที่ Risk calls
- Constraint: Risk must NEVER call `Diag_Init()` or `Log_Init()` directly

---

### P5 — EA_Lifecycle Include Policy Locked

| Rule | Detail |
|------|--------|
| EA_Lifecycle.mqh ห้าม `#include` | Zero include directives — coordinator only |
| EA_TEMPLATE.mq5 owns all includes | Single source of truth สำหรับ include graph |
| EA_Lifecycle contains only | Function calls, state transitions, `g_EA_State` management |
| Enforcement | Code review gate: zero `#include` in EA_Lifecycle |

## Correction 1 — P2: Guard ต้องครอบทั้งไฟล์

**ผิด (ที่ส่งไป):**
```mql5
// ❌ Guard ครอบแค่ constants block แล้วปิดทันที
#ifndef __RISK_CONTRACT__
#define __RISK_CONTRACT__

#define RISK_HALT_DAILY_DD    "DAILY_DD_LIMIT"
...
#define RISK_RESET_SYMBOL_ECONOMICS 4

#endif // __RISK_CONTRACT__    ← ปิดก่อนจบไฟล์

// ... ส่วนที่เหลือของ contract ไม่ได้รับการคุ้มครอง
```

**ถูก:**
```mql5
//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
//| Risk_Contract.mqh                                                |
//| EA_PLATFORM · CORE · Phase 3 · Pattern A Contract               |
//| Version : 1.1 (P2 patch — guard + constants moved)              |
//| Date    : 2026.06.06                                             |
//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
#ifndef __RISK_CONTRACT__          // ← บรรทัดแรกหลัง header comment
#define __RISK_CONTRACT__

// ─── HALT REASON CONSTANTS ───────────────────────────────────────
#define RISK_HALT_DAILY_DD    "DAILY_DD_LIMIT"
#define RISK_HALT_TOTAL_DD    "TOTAL_DD_LIMIT"
#define RISK_HALT_MANUAL      "MANUAL_HALT"
#define RISK_HALT_SYMBOL_ECON "SYMBOL_ECONOMICS_FAIL"
#define RISK_HALT_INIT_FAIL   "RISK_INIT_FAIL"
#define RISK_HALT_INVALID_LOT "INVALID_LOT_RESULT"

// ─── RESET BIT FLAGS (P3) ─────────────────────────────────────────
#define RISK_RESET_TOTAL_HALT       1
#define RISK_RESET_MANUAL_HALT      2
#define RISK_RESET_SYMBOL_ECONOMICS 4

// ─── CONTRACT RULES ──────────────────────────────────────────────
// ... ทุกอย่างตามเดิม ...

// ─── RUNTIME DEPENDENCIES (P4) ───────────────────────────────────
// ... documentation ...

// ─── REQUIRED PUBLIC FUNCTIONS ───────────────────────────────────
// ... ทุก section ตามเดิม ...

#endif // __RISK_CONTRACT__        // ← บรรทัดสุดท้ายของไฟล์
```

**กฎ:** `#ifndef` เปิดที่บรรทัดแรกหลัง header comment — `#endif` ปิดที่บรรทัดสุดท้ายของไฟล์เท่านั้น ทุกอย่างในไฟล์อยู่ระหว่าง guard

   Log_Init(ea_name, symbol);\\n    // Log_Init has no return value \\u2014 failure falls back to Print()\\n    // No halt on Log failure \\u2014 platform continues with Print fallback\\n\\n    // \\u2500\\u2500 Step 2: Diagnostics \\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\n    Diag_Init(ea_name, symbol);\\n    // Diag_Init has no return value \\u2014 failure is non-fatal\\n    // Risk will WARN if Diag not ready (P4 guard in Risk_Init)\\n\\n    // \\u2500\\u2500 Step 3: Risk Engine \\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\n    if(!Risk_Init(symbol, account_bal))\\n    {\\n        Log_Write(\\\&quot;EA\\\&quot;, \\\&quot;ERROR\\\&quot;,\\n            \\\&quot;Init FAILED at Risk_Init\\\&quot; +\\n            \\\&quot;  status=\\\&quot; + Risk_GetInitStatus() +\\n            \\\&quot;  halt=\\\&quot;   + Risk_GetHaltReason());\\n        Diag_SetFlag(\\\&quot;trading_locked\\\&quot;, true);\\n        EA_SetState(state_var, EA_STATE_ERROR,\\n            \\\&quot;Risk_Init failed: \\\&quot; + Risk_GetInitStatus());\\n        return INIT_FAILED;\\n    }\\n\\n    // \\u2500\\u2500 Step 4: Signal Engine placeholder \\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\n    // Phase 5: Signal_Init(symbol) goes here\\n    // if(!Signal_Init(symbol)) { ... EA_STATE_ERROR ... }\\n\\n    // \\u2500\\u2500 Step 5: Execution Engine placeholder \\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\n    // Future: Execution_Init(symbol, magic) goes here\\n\\n    // \\u2500\\u2500 Success \\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\n    EA_SetState(state_var, EA_STATE_READY);\\n\\n    Log_Write(\\\&quot;EA\\\&quot;, \\\&quot;INFO\\\&quot;,\\n        \\\&quot;Init complete\\\&quot; +\\n        \\\&quot;  ea=\\\&quot;     + ea_name +\\n        \\\&quot;  symbol=\\\&quot; + symbol  +\\n        \\\&quot;  magic=\\\&quot;  + IntegerToString(magic) +\\n        \\\&quot;  balance=\\\&quot; + DoubleToString(account_bal, 2) +\\n        (MQLInfoInteger(MQL_TESTER) ? \\\&quot;  [TESTER]\\\&quot; : \\\&quot;  [LIVE]\\\&quot;));\\n\\n    return INIT_SUCCEEDED;\\n}\\n\\n//====================================================================\\n// EA_DeinitAll\\n// Called from OnDeinit() in EA_TEMPLATE.mq5.\\n// Uses deinit reason to decide whether to close positions.\\n// Reverse init order: Risk \\u2192 Diag \\u2192 Log.\\n// Log_Close() is always last.\\n//====================================================================\\nvoid EA_DeinitAll(ENUM_EA_STATE &amp;state_var, const int reason)\\n{\\n    EA_SetState(state_var, EA_STATE_CLOSING,\\n        \\\&quot;reason=\\\&quot; + IntegerToString(reason));\\n\\n    // Determine whether to close positions based on deinit reason\\n    bool close_positions = (reason != REASON_RECOMPILE  &amp;&amp;\\n                            reason != REASON_PARAMETERS &amp;&amp;\\n                            reason != REASON_CHARTCLOSE);\\n    // Note: REASON_CHARTCLOSE closes the whole chart \\u2014 MT5 closes\\n    // positions automatically if account is set to close-on-remove.\\n    // EA does not need to duplicate this. Override per broker policy.\\n\\n    Log_Write(\\\&quot;EA\\\&quot;, \\\&quot;INFO\\\&quot;,\\n        \\\&quot;Deinit starting\\\&quot; +\\n        \\\&quot;  reason=\\\&quot;          + IntegerToString(reason) +\\n        \\\&quot;  close_positions=\\\&quot; + (close_positions ? \\\&quot;Y\\\&quot; : \\\&quot;N\\\&quot;));\\n\\n    // \\u2500\\u2500 Future: Execution_Close(close_positions) \\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\n    // \\u2500\\u2500 Future: Signal_Close() \\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\n\\n    // \\u2500\\u2500 Risk Engine \\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\n    Risk_Close();\\n\\n    // \\u2500\\u2500 Diagnostics \\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\n    // Diag_Close() exports CSV/TXT \\u2014 must be before Log_Close()\\n    Diag_Close();\\n\\n    // \\u2500\\u2500 Logging \\u2014 always last \\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\n    Log_Write(\\\&quot;EA\\\&quot;, \\\&quot;INFO\\\&quot;, \\\&quot;Deinit complete \\u2014 closing log\\\&quot;);\\n    Log_Close();\\n}\\n\\n//====================================================================\\n// EA_OnTimerCycle\\n// Called from OnTimer() in EA_TEMPLATE.mq5.\\n// Runs the full periodic pipeline:\\n//   1. Increment cycle counter\\n//   2. Update risk state with current equity\\n//   3. Check halt \\u2192 set HALTED state if triggered\\n//   4. If HALTED: update chart comment, return\\n//   5. If READY or IN_TRADE: run signal + execution pipeline\\n//\\n// Parameters:\\n//   state_var    : reference to g_EA_State\\n//   current_equity: AccountInfoDouble(ACCOUNT_EQUITY) from caller\\n//====================================================================\\nvoid EA_OnTimerCycle(ENUM_EA_STATE &amp;state_var, double current_equity)\\n{\\n    // Gate: only process in operational states\\n    if(state_var == EA_STATE_ERROR    ||\\n       state_var == EA_STATE_CLOSING  ||\\n       state_var == EA_STATE_INIT     ||\\n       state_var == EA_STATE_UNINITIALIZED)\\n        return;\\n\\n    // \\u2500\\u2500 1. Diagnostic heartbeat \\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\n    Diag_Increment(\\\&quot;timer_cycles\\\&quot;);\\n\\n    // \\u2500\\u2500 2. Risk state update \\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\n    Risk_UpdateState(current_equity);\\n\\n    // \\u2500\\u2500 3. Halt cleared \\u2014 return to READY if was HALTED \\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\n    if(state_var == EA_STATE_HALTED)\\n    {\\n        EA_SetState(state_var, EA_STATE_READY, \\\&quot;halt cleared\\\&quot;);\\n        Diag_SetFlag(\\\&quot;trading_locked\\\&quot;, false);\\n    }\\n\\n    // \\u2500\\u2500 5. Signal evaluation placeholder \\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\u2500\\n    // Phase 5: SignalResult sig = Signal_Evaluate();\\n    // if(sig.direction != 0) { ... Execution_Process(sig) ... }\\n\\n    // \\u2500\\u2500 6.    break;\\n        case EA_STATE_IN_TRADE: state_str = \\\&quot;IN_TRADE\\\&quot;; break;\\n        case EA_STATE_HALTED:   state_str = \\\&quot;HALTED\\\&quot;;   break;\\n        case EA_STATE_ERROR:    state_str = \\\&quot;ERROR\\\&quot;;    break;\\n        default:                state_str = \\\&quot;?\\\&quot;;        break;\\n    }\\n\\n    string halt_str = Risk_CheckHalt()\\n        ? \\\&quot;  HALT: \\\&quot; + Risk_GetHaltReason()\\n        : \\\&quot;\\\&quot;;\\n\\n    string comment =\\n        \\\&quot;EA_PLATFORM v1.0\\\\n\\\&quot; +\\n        \\\&quot;State   : \\\&quot; + state_str + halt_str + \\\&quot;\\\\n\\\&quot; +\\n        \\\&quot;Equity  : \\\&quot; + DoubleToString(current_equity, 2) + \\\&quot;\\\\n\\\&quot; +\\n        \\\&quot;Daily DD: \\\&quot; + DoubleToString(Risk_GetDailyDDPct(), 2) + \\\&quot;%\\\\n\\\&quot; +\\n        \\\&quot;Total DD: \\\&quot; + DoubleToString(Risk_GetTotalDDPct(), 2) + \\\&quot;%\\\\n\\\&quot; +\\n        \\\&quot;Cycles  : \\\&quot; + IntegerToString(Diag_GetCounter(\\\&quot;timer_cycles\\\&quot;)) + \\\&quot;\\\\n\\\&quot; +\\n        \\\&quot;Blocks  : \\\&quot; + IntegerToString(Diag_GetCounter(\\\&quot;entries_blocked\\\&quot;));\\n\\n    Comment(comment);\\n}\\n\\n//====================================================================\\n// EA_IsOperational\\n// Helper for EA_TEMPLATE event handlers to gate logic quickly.\\n// Returns true only when EA is in a state where processing is valid.\\n//====================================================================\\nbool EA_IsOperational(ENUM_EA_STATE state_var)\\n{\\n    return (state_var == EA_STATE_READY   ||\\n            state_var == EA_STATE_IN_TRADE ||\\n            state_var == EA_STATE_HALTED);\\n}\\n\\n#endif // __EA_LIFECYCLE__\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n*EA_Lifecycle.mqh v1.0 | EA_PLATFORM Phase 4 | 2026.06.06*\\n*Zero #include directives \\u2014 P5 policy enforced.*\\n*State machine: UNINITIALIZED \\u2192 INIT \\u2192 READY \\u2192 IN_TRADE/HALTED \\u2192 CLOSING*\\n\&quot;, \&quot;confidentiality_level\&quot;: \&quot;internal\&quot;}&quot;" result="&quot;\n\n---\n### ✅ Ready\n📁 **EA_Lifecycle_1780766189.docx**\n🔗 [Download](https://scgc-ailylab.scg.com/mcp/docsgenerate/files/a3002ad1dadfc05832959e67b32f3a5a.docx?exp=1780802189&amp;sig=7c5f6027930aadec8f844c3730ffebe40e1d3c6388b0afa81dac141125b66570)\n---\n&quot;" files="" embeds="&quot;&quot;">

hasn&#x27;t fired)\\n    g_Current_Bid = SymbolInfoDouble(g_EA_Symbol, SYMBOL_BID);\\n    g_Current_Ask = SymbolInfoDouble(g_EA_Symbol, SYMBOL_ASK);\\n\\n    // Delegate full timer pipeline to EA_Lifecycle\\n    EA_OnTimerCycle(\\n        g_EA_State,\\n        AccountInfoDouble(ACCOUNT_EQUITY)\\n    );\\n}\\n\\n//====================================================================\\n// OnTradeTransaction\\n// Handles position open/close notifications from MT5.\\n// Updates g_EA_State between READY and IN_TRADE based on position count.\\n//====================================================================\\nvoid OnTradeTransaction(\\n    const MqlTradeTransaction &amp;trans,\\n    const MqlTradeRequest     &amp;request,\\n    const MqlTradeResult      &amp;result)\\n{\\n    // Gate: only process in operational states\\n    if(!EA_IsOperational(g_EA_State)) return;\\n\\n    // Update state based on open position count for this magic\\n    int pos_count = 0;\\n    for(int i = 0; i &lt; PositionsTotal(); i++)\\n    {\\n        if(PositionGetTicket(i) &gt; 0)\\n        {\\n            if(PositionGetInteger(POSITION_MAGIC) == inp_EA_Magic &amp;&amp;\\n               PositionGetString(POSITION_SYMBOL) == g_EA_Symbol)\\n            {\\n                pos_count++;\\n            }\\n        }\\n    }\\n\\n    if(pos_count &gt; 0 &amp;&amp; g_EA_State == EA_STATE_READY)\\n    {\\n        EA_SetState(g_EA_State, EA_STATE_IN_TRADE,\\n            \\\&quot;positions=\\\&quot; + IntegerToString(pos_count));\\n        Diag_Increment(\\\&quot;entries_executed\\\&quot;);\\n    }\\n    else if(pos_count == 0 &amp;&amp; g_EA_State == EA_STATE_IN_TRADE)\\n    {\\n        EA_SetState(g_EA_State, EA_STATE_READY,\\n            \\\&quot;all positions closed\\\&quot;);\\n    }\\n\\n    // Log transaction type\\n    if(trans.type == TRADE_TRANSACTION_DEAL_ADD)\\n    {\\n        Log_Write(\\\&quot;EA\\\&quot;, \\\&quot;INFO\\\&quot;,\\n            \\\&quot;TradeTransaction  DEAL_ADD\\\&quot; +\\n            \\\&quot;  ticket=\\\&quot; + IntegerToString((int)trans.deal) +\\n            \\\&quot;  pos_count=\\\&quot; + IntegerToString(pos_count));\\n    }\\n}\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n# FILE 2 \\u2014 ## Phase 4 Build Summary

### EA_Lifecycle.mqh — Key Design Points

```
P5 enforced: zero #include directives — coordinator only

ENUM_EA_STATE:
  UNINITIALIZED → INIT → READY ↔ IN_TRADE
                              ↔ HALTED
                              → ERROR
                              → CLOSING

EA_InitAll():
  Log_Init → Diag_Init → Risk_Init
  Any fail → ERROR state → INIT_FAILED immediately
  Never inits subsequent modules after failure

EA_DeinitAll():
  REASON_RECOMPILE / REASON_PARAMETERS → skip position close
  Risk_Close → Diag_Close → Log_Close (reverse order enforced)
  Log_Close always last

EA_OnTimerCycle():
  ERROR/CLOSING/INIT/UNINITIALIZED → gated out immediately
  READY/IN_TRADE/HALTED → run pipeline
  Halt triggered → transition to HALTED automatically
  Halt cleared → transition back to READY automatically
```

### EA_TEMPLATE.mq5 — Key Design Points

```
Owns entire include graph (P5)
g_EA_State declared here — passed by reference to EA_Lifecycle
OnTick()  → price cache only (Bid/Ask)
OnTimer() → full pipeline via EA_OnTimerCycle()
OnTradeTransaction() → READY ↔ IN_TRADE by position count
__EA_TEMPLATE_INPUTS__ guard → suppresses module-level inputs
```

### ⚠️ One item to verify before compile

`T01_Log_ready` ใน test ใช้ `Log_IsReady()` ซึ่ง **ยังไม่มี getter ใน Logging_v1.mqh** — เพิ่ม getter นี้ก่อน compile หรือ remove assertion นั้น:

```mql5
// เพิ่มใน Logging_v1.mqh ต่อจาก Log_Close():
bool Log_IsReady() { return _Log_Ready; }
```

| First signal module | `Signal_TrendEMA.mqh` — EMA crossover, simple, testable |
| Entry Engine | Phase 5 หรือ Phase 6? — recommend Phase 5 พร้อม Signal |

## PATCH 1 — `#define __EA_TEMPLATE_INPUTS__` ต้องอยู่เหนือ `#include` ทุกบรรทัด

### `TEMPLATE/EA_TEMPLATE.mq5` — Top section แก้เป็น:

```mql5
//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
//| EA_TEMPLATE.mq5                                                  |
//| EA_PLATFORM · TEMPLATE · Phase 4                                 |
//| Version    : 1.0                                                  |
//| Date       : 2026.06.06                                          |
//| AI-GENERATED 2026.06.06 — review before use                      |
//+------------------------------------------------------------------+
#property strict
#property copyright "EA_PLATFORM"
#property version   "1.00"

//====================================================================
// INPUT OWNERSHIP GUARD
// Must be defined BEFORE any #include so module-level input
// declarations inside RiskEngine_v1.mqh (and future modules)
// are suppressed by their #ifndef __EA_TEMPLATE_INPUTS__ guard.
// If this line appears after #include, the guard fires too late —
// module inputs are already declared, causing duplicate errors.
//====================================================================
#define __EA_TEMPLATE_INPUTS__

//====================================================================
// INCLUDES — EA_TEMPLATE.mq5 is the ONLY file that includes modules
// Order: dependencies must precede dependents
// EA_Lifecycle.mqh is last — coordinator, uses all above symbols
//====================================================================
#include "../CORE/Log_Contract.mqh"
#include "../CORE/Logging_v1.mqh"
#include "../CORE/Diagnostics_Contract.mqh"
#include "../CORE/Diagnostics_v1.mqh"
#include "../CORE/Risk_Contract.mqh"
#include "../CORE/RiskEngine_v1.mqh"
// Phase 5: #include "../CORE/Signal_Contract.mqh"
// Phase 5: #include "../SIGNAL/Signal_TrendEMA.mqh"
// Future:  #include "../CORE/EntryEngine_v1.mqh"
// Future:  #include "../CORE/ExitEngine_v1.mqh"
#include "../CORE/EA_Lifecycle.mqh"

// ... inputs, global state, OnInit/OnTick/OnTimer/OnDeinit unchanged
```

**Why order matters:**

```
WRONG — too late:
  #include "RiskEngine_v1.mqh"   ← compiler sees #ifndef __EA_TEMPLATE_INPUTS__
                                    → false → declares inputs → ✓ first time
  #define __EA_TEMPLATE_INPUTS__ ← defined after — useless for this translation unit

CORRECT:
  #define __EA_TEMPLATE_INPUTS__ ← compiler state: symbol defined
  #include "RiskEngine_v1.mqh"   ← compiler sees #ifndef __EA_TEMPLATE_INPUTS__
                                    → true (already defined) → skips inputs ✓
```

---

//====================================================================
// INCLUDES
//====================================================================

//====================================================================

## PATCH 2 — Compile Plan: Remove standalone EA_Lifecycle compile step

### Updated Compile Sequence Table

| Step | File | Location | Expected |
|------|------|----------|----------|
| C1 | `Log_Contract.mqh` | CORE\ | 0 errors, 0 warnings |
| C2 | `Logging_v1.mqh` | CORE\ | 0 errors, 0 warnings |
| C3 | `Diagnostics_Contract.mqh` | CORE\ | 0 errors, 0 warnings |
| C4 | `Diagnostics_v1.mqh` | CORE\ | 0 errors, 0 warnings |
| C5 | `Risk_Contract.mqh` | CORE\ | 0 errors, 0 warnings |
| C6 | `RiskEngine_v1.mqh` | CORE\ | 0 errors, 0 warnings |
| ~~C7~~ | ~~`EA_Lifecycle.mqh` standalone~~ | ~~CORE\~~ | **REMOVED** |
| C7 | `EA_TEMPLATE.mq5` | TEMPLATE\ | 0 errors, 0 warnings — validates EA_Lifecycle indirectly |
| C8 | `EA_TEMPLATE_Test.mq5` | CORE\ | 0 errors, 0 warnings — validates EA_Lifecycle indirectly |

**Rationale for removal:**

`EA_Lifecycle.mqh` has zero `#include` directives (P5 policy). It references `Log_Write()`, `Diag_*()`, `Risk_*()`, and `ENUM_EA_STATE` — none of which exist in its own scope when compiled standalone. Standalone compile of a coordinator-only file that depends on caller scope will always produce `undeclared identifier` errors. The correct validation path is through `EA_TEMPLATE.mq5` and `EA_TEMPLATE_Test.mq5` which provide the full include context.

**Add note to compile plan:**

```
EA_Lifecycle.mqh is a coordinator — it must NOT be compiled standalone.
Validation is via EA_TEMPLATE.mq5 (C7) and EA_TEMPLATE_Test.mq5 (C8).
If MetaEditor highlights EA_Lifecycle.mqh with errors when opened directly,
this is expected and correct — it requires the EA_TEMPLATE include context.
```

---

## PATCH 3 — Add `Log_IsReady()` getter to `Logging_v1.mqh`

Add immediately after `Log_Close()` function, before `#endif // __LOGGING_V1__`:

```mql5
//--------------------------------------------------------------------
// Log_IsReady
// Returns true if Log_Init() has been called successfully and
// Log_Close() has not yet been called.
// Safe to call at any time. Never logs. Never modifies state.
// Added: Phase 4 patch — required by EA_TEMPLATE_Test T01.
//--------------------------------------------------------------------
bool Log_IsReady()
{
    return _Log_Ready;
}
```

**File structure after patch:**

```
Logging_v1.mqh
  #ifndef __LOGGING_V1__
  #define __LOGGING_V1__
  #include "Log_Contract.mqh"
  [static vars]
  [internal helpers]
  void Log_Init(...)   { ... }
  void Log_Write(...)  { ... }
  void Log_Close()     { ... }
  bool Log_IsReady()   { return _Log_Ready; }   ← NEW
  #endif // __LOGGING_V1__
```

# REVIEW — P1–P5 Patch Consistency
## EA_CORE_V1 Pre-Phase 4 Candidate

---

## 1. Are P1–P5 Logically Consistent?

### P1 — `Log_Init()` handle leak fix

**Consistent. No conflicts.**

Logic: close existing handle before opening new one. This is a pure defensive addition — same function signature, same behavior on first call, safer behavior on re-call. No downstream module is affected because `_Log_Handle` is internal to `Logging_v1.mqh`. Nothing outside reads this variable directly.

One subtlety to verify: `Log_Init()` is called before `Diag_Init()` and `Risk_Init()` in every init sequence. If P1's handle-close path runs and `FileClose()` receives `INVALID_HANDLE`, MQL5 silently ignores it — no error. Safe.

**Verdict: P1 is self-contained and consistent with all other patches.**

---

### P2 — Move `RISK_HALT_*` constants to `Risk_Contract.mqh`

**Consistent. One sequencing requirement that must be respected.**

The move is logically correct. `Risk_Contract.mqh` has no dependencies — it is a pure definition file. Adding `#define` constants there does not create any new include dependency. Any module that currently resolves these constants via `RiskEngine_v1.mqh` (because it already includes the full implementation) continues to work — the constants are now just resolved one include earlier in the chain.

The `#ifndef __RISK_CONTRACT__` guard wrapping the entire file is critical. Without it, if `Risk_Contract.mqh` is included by both `Diagnostics_v1.mqh` (future) and `RiskEngine_v1.mqh`, and both are included by `EA_TEMPLATE.mq5`, the constants would be defined twice. MQL5 `#define` redefinition is a compile error in `#property strict` mode. The guard prevents this.

**Sequencing requirement:** P2 must be applied atomically — add to `Risk_Contract.mqh` AND remove from `RiskEngine_v1.mqh` in the same edit. If only one side is done:

| Half-applied state | Result |
|-------------------|--------|
| Added to contract only, still in implementation | Duplicate `#define` → compile error |
| Removed from implementation only, not in contract | `RISK_HALT_*` undeclared in test file → compile error |

**Verdict: P2 is consistent if applied atomically. Guard must wrap entire file.**

---

### P3 — `Risk_ManualReset()` named bit-flag API

**Consistent. Requires complete project-wide call site sweep.**

The signature change from `(bool, bool, bool)` to `(int flags)` is a breaking change to the public API. MQL5 does not have function overloading in the same way — the old signature becomes a compile error at every call site that passes three booleans. This is the correct behavior: it forces all callers to be updated explicitly rather than silently passing wrong values.

`RISK_RESET_TOTAL_HALT`, `RISK_RESET_MANUAL_HALT`, and `RISK_RESET_SYMBOL_ECONOMICS` are defined in `Risk_Contract.mqh` (P2 applied first). Since P3 depends on P2 for the constant definitions, **P2 must be applied before P3**. This ordering is already specified in the patch plan and is correct.

Known call sites in current codebase:

| File | Section | Risk |
|------|---------|------|
| `RiskEngine_v1_Test.mq5` | T07, T10, T11, T15, T22 | All must be updated |
| `EA_TEMPLATE_Test.mq5` | T07, T11 | All must be updated |
| `EA_Lifecycle.mqh` | Any future `Risk_ManualReset` call | Must use new API |
| `RiskEngine_v1.mqh` | Implementation itself | Signature change only — internal logic unchanged |

**Verdict: P3 is consistent. Depends on P2. Complete project sweep is mandatory — partial update causes compile failure.**

---

### P4 — Runtime dependency documentation in `Risk_Contract.mqh`

**Consistent. Documentation only — zero runtime effect.**

Adding a documentation section to `Risk_Contract.mqh` does not change any compiled output. It has no interaction with any other patch. The only risk is if a future reviewer misreads it as a compile-time dependency (it is not — it documents a call-order constraint, not an `#include` chain).

The most important point in the documentation is: *"RiskEngine must NEVER call `Diag_Init()` or `Log_Init()` internally."* This is a behavioral constraint that cannot be enforced by the compiler — it is enforced by code review only. P4 makes this explicit.

**Verdict: P4 is consistent and has no conflict with any other patch.**

---

### P5 — EA_Lifecycle include policy lock

**Consistent. Structural constraint, not a code change.**

P5 states that `EA_Lifecycle.mqh` must contain zero `#include` directives. The Phase 4 implementation of `EA_Lifecycle.mqh` already satisfies this. P5 formalizes it as a platform rule in `PLATFORM_RULES.md` and as a header comment in `EA_Lifecycle.mqh` itself.

The consequence of P5 is that `EA_Lifecycle.mqh` cannot be compiled standalone in MetaEditor — it references symbols (`Log_Write`, `Diag_Init`, `Risk_Init`, etc.) that exist only when the full include chain from `EA_TEMPLATE.mq5` is in scope. This is expected and correct. The compile plan correctly removes the standalone compile step for `EA_Lifecycle.mqh`.

**One risk:** if a future developer adds an `#include` to `EA_Lifecycle.mqh` without reading the policy, they introduce a secondary include chain that duplicates the module includes already in `EA_TEMPLATE.mq5`. The `#ifndef` guards on all module files protect against duplicate static declarations, but the violation itself would go undetected by the compiler. P5's enforcement is code review only.

**Verdict: P5 is consistent. No runtime risk. Enforcement is review-gate only.**

---

## 2. Dependency Conflicts Between Modules

Full dependency graph after all patches applied:

```
Log_Contract.mqh          (no deps — definitions only)
  └── Logging_v1.mqh      (← Log_Contract)

Diagnostics_Contract.mqh  (no deps — definitions only)
  └── Diagnostics_v1.mqh  (← Diagnostics_Contract, Logging_v1)

Risk_Contract.mqh         (no deps — definitions + constants + reset flags)
  └── RiskEngine_v1.mqh   (← Risk_Contract, Logging_v1, Diagnostics_v1)

EA_Lifecycle.mqh          (zero includes — coordinator, uses caller scope)

EA_TEMPLATE.mq5           (includes all above in dependency order)
  order: Log_Contract → Logging_v1 → Diagnostics_Contract →
         Diagnostics_v1 → Risk_Contract → RiskEngine_v1 →
         EA_Lifecycle
```

**No circular dependencies.** The graph is a strict DAG. Each node's dependencies are resolved before it is processed.

**Potential conflict — include chain depth in EA_TEMPLATE:**

`Logging_v1.mqh` is included by `EA_TEMPLATE.mq5` directly AND transitively via `Diagnostics_v1.mqh` AND via `RiskEngine_v1.mqh`. That is three include paths to the same file. Without `#ifndef __LOGGING_V1__` guard, this produces duplicate static variable declarations — a compile error. The guard is present. ✓

Same applies to `Diagnostics_v1.mqh`: included directly and via `RiskEngine_v1.mqh`. Guard `#ifndef __DIAGNOSTICS_V1__` is present. ✓

`Risk_Contract.mqh` after P2 has guard `#ifndef __RISK_CONTRACT__` wrapping the entire file. It is included directly by `EA_TEMPLATE.mq5` and transitively via `RiskEngine_v1.mqh`. Guard prevents double-define of `RISK_HALT_*` constants. ✓

**No dependency conflicts found after P1–P5 applied.**

---

## 3. Risk of Moving `RISK_HALT_*` to `Risk_Contract.mqh` Breaking Tests

### Test files that reference `RISK_HALT_*` constants:

| File | Constants used |
|------|---------------|
| `RiskEngine_v1_Test.mq5` | `RISK_HALT_DAILY_DD`, `RISK_HALT_TOTAL_DD`, `RISK_HALT_INIT_FAIL` |
| `EA_TEMPLATE_Test.mq5` | `RISK_HALT_DAILY_DD`, all six constants in T12 |

### How these constants reach test files:

**Before P2:**
```
RiskEngine_v1_Test.mq5
  #include "RiskEngine_v1.mqh"    ← constants defined here
    → RISK_HALT_DAILY_DD resolved ✓
```

**After P2:**
```
RiskEngine_v1_Test.mq5
  #include "RiskEngine_v1.mqh"
    #include "Risk_Contract.mqh"  ← constants defined here now
    → RISK_HALT_DAILY_DD resolved ✓ (via transitive include)
```

The constants are still accessible to test files — the resolution path is one level deeper in the include chain, but the result is identical. Test files include `RiskEngine_v1.mqh` which includes `Risk_Contract.mqh`. The constants are in scope.

**`EA_TEMPLATE_Test.mq5` — T12 explicitly tests constant values:**

```mql5
_Assert("T12_DAILY_DD_constant", RISK_HALT_DAILY_DD == "DAILY_DD_LIMIT");
```

This test verifies that the constant resolves and has the correct string value. After P2, the constant resolves from `Risk_Contract.mqh` instead of `RiskEngine_v1.mqh`. The string value is identical. T12 continues to pass. ✓

**One scenario that would break tests:**

If P2 is half-applied — constants removed from `RiskEngine_v1.mqh` but not yet added to `Risk_Contract.mqh` — then `RISK_HALT_DAILY_DD` is undeclared in all test files. This is a compile error, not a runtime failure. It is immediately visible. The fix is completing P2.

**Verdict: Moving constants does not break any test. Resolution path changes, string values and behavior are identical.**

---

## 4. What Codex Must NOT Change

These are the hard constraints Codex must be briefed on before any implementation:

### Never change:

| Constraint | Reason |
|-----------|--------|
| `#ifndef __LOGGING_V1__` guard location and name | Removing or renaming breaks double-include protection across 3 include paths |
| `#ifndef __DIAGNOSTICS_V1__` guard | Same — 2 include paths |
| `#ifndef __RISK_CONTRACT__` guard wrapping ENTIRE file | Must wrap everything — partial guard (around constants block only) leaves rest of file unprotected |
| `#ifndef __EA_TEMPLATE_INPUTS__` guard in RiskEngine_v1.mqh | Must remain — EA_TEMPLATE defines this before includes to suppress module-level inputs |
| `#define __EA_TEMPLATE_INPUTS__` position in EA_TEMPLATE.mq5 and EA_TEMPLATE_Test.mq5 | Must be above ALL `#include` lines — not after |
| `static` keyword on all `_Log_*`, `_Diag_*`, `_Risk_*` variables | Removing `static` makes them global across translation units — linker errors |
| `Log_Close()` position in deinit sequence | Must always be the last call — modules log during their own close routines |
| `EA_Lifecycle.mqh` — zero `#include` directives | P5 policy — adding includes creates secondary dependency chain |
| `Risk_ManualReset(int flags)` signature | P3 applied — any revert to `(bool, bool, bool)` breaks all call sites |
| `_Risk_NormalizeLot()` round-DOWN behavior | Phase 3 patch — must never use `MathMax(lot, vol_min)` to raise lot |

### Never add:

| Do not add | Reason |
|-----------|--------|
| `OrderSend()` outside `ExecutionEngine_v1.mqh` | Platform rule — only Execution Engine sends orders |
| `#include` inside `EA_Lifecycle.mqh` | P5 policy violation |
| Direct access to `_Diag_*` or `_Risk_*` or `_Log_*` internal vars from outside owning file | Pattern A rule — all access via public API only |
| New `input` declarations in any `.mqh` file without `#ifndef __EA_TEMPLATE_INPUTS__` guard | Causes duplicate input errors when EA_TEMPLATE owns all inputs |
| `Diag_Init()` or `Log_Init()` calls inside any module's own init function | P4 documented constraint — lifecycle orchestration belongs to EA_Lifecycle only |

---

### Phase 4 — EA_TEMPLATE + EA_Lifecycle

```
EA_Lifecycle.mqh          Coordinator — zero #include (P5 policy)
EA_TEMPLATE.mq5           Master EA skeleton
EA_TEMPLATE_Test.mq5      45 assertions
```

**EA State Machine:**
```
UNINITIALIZED → INIT → READY ↔ IN_TRADE
                            ↔ HALTED
                            → ERROR
                            → CLOSING
```

**Key design decisions:**
- `OnTick()` = price cache only (Bid/Ask)
- `OnTimer()` = full pipeline (Risk → Signal → Execution)
- `g_EA_State` owned by `EA_TEMPLATE.mq5` — passed by reference to Lifecycle
- `#define __EA_TEMPLATE_INPUTS__` ต้องอยู่เหนือ `#include` ทุกบรรทัด
- `REASON_RECOMPILE` และ `REASON_PARAMETERS` ไม่ปิด position

**เพิ่ม `Log_IsReady()` getter** ใน `Logging_v1.mqh` ก่อน compile

## Architecture Principles ที่ยึดตลอด Session

| Principle | Detail |
|-----------|--------|
| **Pattern A** | Struct + function naming convention — ไม่ใช้ C#/Java interface |
| **Pattern C** | Include-file contract สำหรับ module ที่ไม่มี struct dependency |
| **Dependency flows one way** | Logging ← Diagnostics ← Risk ← Lifecycle ← Template |
| **No upward lot rounding** | `MathFloor` เท่านั้น — reject ถ้า < vol_min |
| **Risk Engine bypass = PROHIBITED** | Platform rule ไม่มีข้อยกเว้น |
| **DRY_RUN default true** | ไม่มี real trade จนกว่าจะ enable explicit |
| **EA_Lifecycle = coordinator only** | Zero `#include` — P5 policy |
| **EA_TEMPLATE owns include graph** | Single source of truth สำหรับทุก include |
| **One module per Codex session** | Multi-module session = inconsistent quality |
| **Architecture quality > code quantity** | Claude review ก่อน Codex implement เสมอ |
